8.6 Atomic Energy (Radiation) Protection Rules, 2004  
As per Rule 3 of the Atomic Energy (Radiation Protection) Rules, 2004 issued by 
Department of Atomic Energy , GoI, no person shall, without a licence establish or 
decomm ission a radiation installation  and no person shall handle any radioactive 
material, or operate any radiation generating equipment except in accordance with the 
terms and conditions of the licence.   
Contrary to the  provisions of the said Rules, A udit noticed  that requisite  license  from  
Atomic Energy Regulatory Board  (AERB ) had not been  obtained  in two23 out of six 
selected DHs, 1224 out of selected 18 SDHs and eight25 out of 10 selected CHCs where 
radiation generating equipment were available  as of March 2022 . 
Rule 19 read with Rule 22 of the Atomic Energy (Radiation Protection) Rules, 2004 
provides for appointment and responsibilities of a Radiological Safety Officer (RSO) 
who shall be  responsible for advising and assisting the employer and licensee on safety 
aspects aimed at ensuring that the provisions of the Atomic Energy (Radiation 
Protection) Rules, 2004 are complied with. The RSO shall also carry out rout ine 
measurements and analysis of  radiation and radioactivity  levels in the controlled area 
and report  on all hazardous situations along with details of any immediate remedial 
actions etc.  
Audit observed that the post of Radiological Safety Officer was not existing in the 
Department. Thus, due to non -availability of RSO in the Department, the unnecessary 
exposure either due to equipment malfunction or human error remained unnoticed 
which may be harmful for the patients as well as the staff operating the equipment.  
The Chief Accounts Officer, Directorate of Health Services , Jammu and Dy. Director 
Health Services , Kashmir admitted (January 2025) that no post of Radiological Safety 
Officer is existing in the Department.   
 
 
23   DHs Ganderbal and Handwara  
24   Anantnag: four ; Jammu : five and Kupwara : three  
25   Jammu : three ; Kupwara : three  and Ramban : two  


Chapter VIII: Adequacy and effectiveness of regulatory mechanism  
195 8.7 Concl usion  
The envisaged regulatory mechanism was not functioning effectively to ensure 
responsible provision of health services to the people. No targets for inspections under 
the D and C Act, 1940 were fixed. Th ere was lack of mechanism where -under drug 
samples were to be referred to SDC for testing on regular basis. There was delay in 
issue of notification for nomination of MDTOs. No Apex body (UT Council) was 
constituted by the GoJ&K under Clinical Establishments (Registration and 
Regulation), Rules, 2020 to ensure  that the appeals against cases of de -registration and 
other penalties levied by the District Registering Authority can be heard.  For the 
registration and regulation of the clinical establishments, no survey was conducted to 
ascertain the actual number of CEs eligible for registration. There was disparity in rates 
of various pathological/  radiological tests. Requisite annual reports were not submitted 
by some  of the healthcare facilities under BMW  Management Rules, 2016  and 
authorisation  was not obtained by the occupier or operator handling BMW.  
8.8 Recommendations  
In view  of the audit findings, GoJ&K may consider:  
• Fixing of targets for inspections under the D &C Act, 1940 to ensure adequate 
regulatory control;  
• Referring drug samples to SDC for testing on regular basis ; 
• Issuing  notification for nomination of MDTOs at the earliest so that the 
samples of medical devices are got tested from the notified MDTLs ;  
• Constituting a UT Council as required under Clinical Establishments 
(Registration and Regulation), Rules, 2020 to ensure that the appeals against 
cases of de -registration and other penalties levied by the District Registering 
Authority can be heard;  
• Framing a mechanism to ascertain the actual number of CEs eligible for 
registration so that CEs operating without r egistration c an be identified ; and  
• Ensuring compliance with Bio -Medical Waste Management Rules by all 
healthcare facilities  with regard to obtaining requisite authorisation, 
submission of annual returns  etc. 

 
  
CHAPTER -IX 
SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT GOAL 3  

 
 
 
  

 
197 CHAPTER -IX 
Sustainable Development Goal 3 
With the aim of taking forward the success of Millennium Development Goals, the 
United Nations (UN) General Assembly, in its 70th session held on 25th September , 
2015 adopted the document titled “Transforming our World: the 2030 Agenda for 
Sustainable Development”. The document consisted of 17 Sustainable Development 
Goals (SDGs) and associated 169 targets which came into force with effect from  
1st January , 2016. India was also a signatory to the agenda.  
In India, National Institution for Transforming India (NITI) Aayog is responsible for 
overall coordination of the SDGs and the Ministry of Statistics and Programme 
Implementation (MoSPI) is responsible for the formulation of the National Indicator 
Framewor k (NIF) to monitor the SDGs.  
9.1 Formulation of State Indicator Framework and District Indicator 
 Framework  
On the analogy of National Indicator Framework, GoJ&K prepared (October 2019) UT 
Indicator Framework (UTIF) which was updated from time to time. At the national 
level  
Goal -3 has 13 targets and 41 indicators. GoJ&K identified 12 targets1 and 34 indicators 
under Goal 3  (Sustainable Development Goal 3, regardi ng "Good Health and  
Well -Being") .  
A brief description of the 12 targets and 34 indicators under Goal 3 is given in  
Table 9.1 .

9.1.1  Tier-wise indicator for SDG 3 
The Global Indicator Framework (GIF) for Sustainable Development Goals developed 
by the Inter -Agency and Expert Group on SDG Indicators (IAEG2-SDGs) comprises 
231 indicators. Based on the level of methodological development and the availability 
of data, all these indicators have been classified into three tiers (Tier3 I, II and III) to 
facilitate implementation. As of 4th February 2022, there were 25 indicators under  
Tier I, 03 under Tier II for SDG 3. For Tier III, methodology and standards  are yet to 
be specified.

population in every region where the indicator is relevant.

Number of deaths due to cancer, (iii) 3.8.2 -Proportion of population with large household expenditures on 
health as a share of total household expenditure or income, (iv) 3.a.1 -Percentage of adults 15 years and above 
with use o f any kind of  tobacco, (v) 3.b.2 -Budgetary allocation for Department of Health Research and  
(vi) 3.d.1 -International Health Regulations (IHR) capacity and health emergency preparedness . 

Chapter IX: Sustainable Development Goal 3  
199 9.1.2  Sustainable Development Goal 3 and its adoption in J&K  
SDG 3 envisions ensuring healthy lives and promotion of well -being for all at all ages. 
The Goal addresses all major health priorities, including reproductive, maternal and 
child health; communicable, non -communicable and environmental diseases; universal 
health coverage; and access to safe, effective, quality and affordable medicines and 
vaccines. In addition, the new National Health Policy (NHP) 2017 is set in the context 
of SDGs. GoJ&K set up (June 2018) a State/ UT Level Committee for achievement of 
SDG s under the chairmanship of the Chief Secretary.  
9.1.2.1  Planning an d mapping budget for SDG 3 
The National Health Policy 2017 envisaged allocation of resources for healthcare in 
accordance with development and financial indicators in a State/ UT. Audit observed 
that expenditure on public health decreased from 2.95 in 2020 -21 to 2.80 per cent in 
2021 -22 of the GSDP. However, no action plan for achieving SDGs was formulated by 
the Department.  
Though NITI Aayog had mapped Centrally Sponsored Schemes with the SDG targets, 
as of March 2022, no information was provided by the Planning, Development and 
Monitoring Department5 in respect of interlinkages between goals and targets and 
mapping of schemes to SDG  3 though  called for by Audit. Due to non -availability of 
the requisite information, the extent of integration of SDG 3 into the accounting and 
budgeting framework could not be ascertained by Audit.  
Department of Health and Medical Education, Government of Jammu and Kashmir 
constituted  (August 2022) an Inter -Sectoral Committee6 for setting up of “Institutional 
Framework for improvement of health services with special focus on Sustainable 
Development Goals (SDGs), District Good Governance Index (DGGI) and Vision 
JK@2047”. The aim was to strategise the policies, programmes and sta ndards for 
achievement of SDG 3 to improve important health indicators by aligning the policies/ 
action plans with mapping of various schemes . The Committee was to meet once in a 
month to review the progress made with quantifiable targets and achievements on SDG. 
Audit noticed that the first meeting of the Committee was held in December 2023  i.e. 
more than 15 months after its constitution . 
9.2 Health Indicators  
The SDG India Index and Dashboard of NITI Aayog tracks the progress of the country 
as well as the States and UTs compositely on all SDGs as well as on each individual 
SDG. Every year, the indicators are reviewed and updated in view of evolving 
availability of data, and the index is computed to capture t he individual as well as 
collective momentum on implementation of SDGs at State/  UT and national levels.  
 
5  Being the nodal department for recording the progress/designing targets on all indicators set under SDGs.  
6  Comprising of Mission Director, National Health Mission, J&K -Chairman, Director General, Family Welfare, 
MCH and Immunisation -Co-chairman, Chief Executive Officer, State Health Agency -Member, Director 
Health Services, Jammu/  Kashmir -Members, Director Planning, NHM, J&K -Member, Programme Manager 
HMIS, NHM, J&K -Member Secretary and Representatives of the GMCs to be nominated by the respective 
Principals as Members.

9.3 Conclusion  
UT of J&K adopted 34 indicators out of 41 indicators at the national level covering 12 
targets at UT level out of 13 targets at national level. As of December 2023, t he Inter -
Sectoral Committee  only met once since  its constitution.  
9.4 Recommendation  
In light of the audit findings, GoJ&K may consider:  
• Ensuring Inter -Sectoral Committee meetings are held at regular intervals
to monitor the progress and achievements of targets under SDG .
